I mean, I don’t usually vomit or do anything to try to get the food out after.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Since early in the semester, it’s been happening most weeks. Usually it’s one night, but sometimes two, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady like that for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Afterward I feel really embarrassed, and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so when this happens it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and I feel ashamed about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely, and I haven’t noticed a big change in my clothes or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like after a project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll end up eating way more than a normal dinner. I’ve had nights where I ate a whole small pizza, then chips, then cereal in my room. It’s a lot more than I meant to eat, and more than I think my roommates would eat in that same situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake to try to make up for the amount you ate during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I might tell myself I should eat “better” the next day, but I don’t really fast or do anything extreme to make up for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed, especially after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m depressed all the time, but after it happens I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so it makes me feel like I’m not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food without trying to purge or compensate for them in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much what happens. Like on nights after a big project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza around, I’ll eat way more than I meant to—sometimes a whole small pizza, then chips and cereal in my room. Once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some kind of switch, and even though part of me is thinking, “Dennis, stop,” I just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually vomit or try to get rid of the food after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t usually vomit or do anything to try to get the food out after.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Since early in the semester, it’s been happening most weeks. Usually it’s one night, but sometimes two, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady like that for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Afterward I feel really embarrassed, and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so when this happens it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and I feel ashamed about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely, and I haven’t noticed a big change in my clothes or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like after a project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll end up eating way more than a normal dinner. I’ve had nights where I ate a whole small pizza, then chips, then cereal in my room. It’s a lot more than I meant to eat, and more than I think my roommates would eat in that same situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake to try to make up for the amount you ate during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I might tell myself I should eat “better” the next day, but I don’t really fast or do anything extreme to make up for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed, especially after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m depressed all the time, but after it happens I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so it makes me feel like I’m not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food without trying to purge or compensate for them in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much what happens. Like on nights after a big project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza around, I’ll eat way more than I meant to—sometimes a whole small pizza, then chips and cereal in my room. And I’m not doing anything after to throw up or make up for it.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen on average?\n\nPatient: Probably most weeks it’s like once a week. Sometimes it happens twice, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady since early in the semester, so… at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week on average?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s about once a week, but some weeks it is more than once, like two nights if deadlines are piling up or there’s a lot of food in the apartment. That’s been pretty steady since early in the semester, maybe three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: On average, do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week?\n\nPatient: I mean, on average it’s probably around once a week. Some weeks it’s two times, like when deadlines hit or there’s a lot of food in the apartment. That’s been going on pretty steadily since early in the semester, maybe three or four months.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

I mean, I don’t usually vomit or do anything to try to get the food out after.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Since early in the semester, it’s been happening most weeks. Usually it’s one night, but sometimes two, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady like that for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Afterward I feel really embarrassed, and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so when this happens it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and I feel ashamed about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely, and I haven’t noticed a big change in my clothes or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like after a project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll end up eating way more than a normal dinner. I’ve had nights where I ate a whole small pizza, then chips, then cereal in my room. It’s a lot more than I meant to eat, and more than I think my roommates would eat in that same situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake to try to make up for the amount you ate during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I might tell myself I should eat “better” the next day, but I don’t really fast or do anything extreme to make up for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed, especially after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m depressed all the time, but after it happens I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so it makes me feel like I’m not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food without trying to purge or compensate for them in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much what happens. Like on nights after a big project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza around, I’ll eat way more than I meant to—sometimes a whole small pizza, then chips and cereal in my room. And I’m not doing anything after to throw up or make up for it.\n\nDoctor: How many times a week do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen on average?\n\nPatient: Probably most weeks it’s like once a week. Sometimes it happens twice, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady since early in the semester, so… at least the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week on average?\n\nPatient: Usually it’s about once a week, but some weeks it is more than once, like two nights if deadlines are piling up or there’s a lot of food in the apartment. That’s been pretty steady since early in the semester, maybe three or four months now.\n\nDoctor: On average, do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week?\n\nPatient: I mean, on average it’s probably around once a week. Some weeks it’s two times, like when deadlines hit or there’s a lot of food in the apartment. That’s been going on pretty steadily since early in the semester, maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week most weeks?\n\nPatient: Most weeks it’s one night, not usually more than that. But some weeks it does happen twice, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. That’s been the pattern since early in the semester, for at least three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen more than once a week most of the time?\n\nPatient: Most of the time it’s more like one night a week. It’s been pretty steady like that since early in the semester, for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen at least twice a week most weeks?\n\nPatient: Not really most weeks, no. That’s really the main thing. Once I start eating like that, it feels like I hit some kind of switch, and even though part of me is thinking, “Dennis, stop,” I just keep going.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually vomit or try to get rid of the food after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I don’t usually vomit or do anything to try to get the food out after.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: Since early in the semester, it’s been happening most weeks. Usually it’s one night, but sometimes two, especially if I’ve got deadlines or there’s a lot of food around the apartment. It’s been pretty steady like that for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty or ashamed after these episodes of eating are over?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Afterward I feel really embarrassed, and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so when this happens it makes me feel like I’m not in control, and I feel ashamed about that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I haven’t been tracking it closely, and I haven’t noticed a big change in my clothes or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period under similar circumstances?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. Like after a project deadline, or if there’s leftover pizza in the apartment, I’ll end up eating way more than a normal dinner. I’ve had nights where I ate a whole small pizza, then chips, then cereal in my room. It’s a lot more than I meant to eat, and more than I think my roommates would eat in that same situation.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever fast or severely restrict your food intake to try to make up for the amount you ate during one of these episodes?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I might tell myself I should eat “better” the next day, but I don’t really fast or do anything extreme to make up for it.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed, especially after these episodes of eating?\n\nPatient: Yeah. I wouldn’t say I’m depressed all the time, but after it happens I feel really embarrassed and kind of grossed out with myself. I’m usually pretty disciplined with school and robotics, so it makes me feel like I’m not in control.\n\nDoctor: Do you have these episodes of eating large amounts of food without trying to purge or compensate for them in any way?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that’s pretty much what happens. That’s been pretty steady since early in the semester, for the last three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen at least three times a week?\n\nPatient: No, not three times a week. It’s mostly been one night a week, sometimes two if deadlines are bad or there’s a lot of food around. That’s been the pattern since early in the semester, for maybe three or four months.\n\nDoctor: Do these episodes of eating large amounts of food happen at least twice a week for most of the past three months?\n\nPatient: Not twice a week for most of the past three months, no.
